Examples from recent years include a series on young people proposing solutions for climate change and a podcast on a Monitor foreign correspondent trying to find hope in Israel and the Palestinian territories after Hamas attacked Israel on Oct. 7 , 2023 . <p> " I put a little sticky on my computer terminal that said , ' CSM stories : look for love , ' " said Francine Kiefer , a former West Coast bureau chief who retired last year . <p> Historically , the paper is well-known for strong international coverage , including its Pulitzer-winning coverage that uncovered the Srebrenica genocide in Bosnia . It 's also been heralded as a level-headed source of domestic news , such as Bryant 's award-winning congressional reporting that included stories about diversifying congressional staff and how staffers who worked on the Watergate hearings felt about the Jan. 6 hearings . <p> Advertisement <p> " It is one of the very few -- if not @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ publications that has a nonbiased view , " said Linda Fuller , an emeritus professor of communications at Worcester State University , who wrote a book on the Monitor in 2011 . <p> But the Monitor 's brand of analytical , progress-driven journalism has n't fit neatly into a modern media landscape that rewards high engagement and virality . <p> After becoming the first national paper to cut daily print , the Monitor initially found success andreached 25 million monthly page views in 2010 . Other outlets quickly caught up , and Monitor leaders began to have concerns that chasing the buzz was undercutting its mission . In 2017 , it turned to building a digital subscription business . It was successful out of the gate , but subscriptions have declined in recent years . The Monitor averaged just over 750,000 monthly page views during the last three months of 2025 . <p> In recent years , the Monitor also experimented with specialized sections such as Passcode , which focused on cybersecurity . F -- the most severe heat wave the country has experienced in 16 years . <p> It 's difficult to outright blame climate change for any one specific weather event , but as our planet warms , it could mean that extremes of all kinds , occurring at the same time around the world , could become the norm . <p> " You ca n't really attribute any specific single weather event to climate change , " says Gary Lackmann , professor in the department of Marine , Earth and Atmospheric Sciences at NC State University . That said , scientists are getting better at determining the degree to which climate change can make @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ are released into the atmosphere , they trap heat , causing air and ocean temperatures to warm and contributing to shifting weather patterns . As Lackmann puts it : " Climate change loads the dice a little bit towards more extreme events . " <p> From hot days to snow storms , hurricanes and droughts , extreme weather events have always been natural . " We 've had extreme weather as long as we have records of weather information , " says Lackmann . But research shows that climate change is making them more frequent and intense . " What we 're finding is that the intensity and the frequency of the most extreme events is certainly likely to have the DNA of climate change , " says Marshall Shepherd , director of the Atmospheric Sciences Program at the University of Georgia and former president of the American Meteorological Society . <p> Advertisement <p> In the United States alone , the frequency of U.S. billion-dollar disasters has increased dramatically since the 1980s -- the country saw about three events annually during the 1980s , compared to 20 events annually during @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ are able to quantify climate change 's influence on an individual weather event , show that climate change is playing a role in supercharging devastating and deadly weather events . A study from World Weather Attribution found that global warming made the January 2025 fires in Los Angeles 35% more likely . And the heat wave that hit the Pacific Northwest in summer 2021 , during which temperatures reached 120 ? F , would have been " virtually impossible " without human-caused climate change , according to a report from Copernicus . World Weather Attribution also analyzed the heat in Australia earlier this month and found that extreme temperatures from the 5th to 10th were made 1.6 ? C hotter due to climate change . <p> Advertisement <p> A warmer planet means we 're more likely to see extremes -- whether that be heat domes that send temperatures soaring for days to languid hurricanes that leave more water in their wake . <p> Warmer ocean temperatures can lead to stronger storms . " When you have really warm water adjacent to really cold land , there 's a really big @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ their energy from that temperature contrast . " <p> As average winter temperatures warm faster than any other season , we could see changes in the type of precipitation that falls . If temperatures rise above freezing , precipitation that once might have occurred as snow could come down as rain instead . A warmer atmosphere is also able to hold more moisture , which can lead to heavier rainfall , worse flooding , or even intense snow storms . <p> " Storms are a natural part of Earth 's system and are not going away , " William Ripple , co-lead author of the 2025 State of the Climate report , told TIME in an email . " We are not losing storms ; we are getting storms that are supercharged with extra water and energy . " <p> Advertisement <p> Meanwhile , hotter days are also becoming more frequent -- the last 11 years have been the hottest 11 years on record . <p> " Yes , you probably can get a heat wave naturally , but in this climate change era , they 're on steroids , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ that slowing down . The latest World Meteorological Organization Global Annual to Decadal Climate Update predicts that Earth 's temperatures are likely to stay at or near record levels from 2025 to 2029 . <p> " What used to be a rare , unusually hot day is now much more common , becoming part of our new normal weather , " says Ripple . " Because of this , heat waves are happening more often , lasting longer , and reaching higher temperatures . " <p> What remains clear is that frigid temperatures and piles of snow by no means disproves climate change . " People will say things like , ' Hey , it 's snowing . That must mean there 's no such thing as global warming . ' " says Shepherd . " I said , ' No , that just means it 's January in the wintertime . ' "

@@133951516 <h> El Nino on the horizon during monsoon months <h> IMD says there is " probability " of an El Nino , but no clarity till April ; " early " climate models signal a likely El Nino in 2026 , raising the risk of a " sub-par monsoon and drought conditions over India , " says Skymet <p> An El Nino is defined as the Central Equatorial Pacific heating , on average , at least half a degree warmer than the average for five consecutive overlapping three-month periods . Photo Credit : Getty Images/iStockphoto <p> There is a chance that the El Nino phenomonen -- a warming of the Central Pacific Ocean frequently linked to weak monsoon rainfall in India -- may occur after July this year , but clarity will only emerge in April , according to M. Mohapatra , director-general of the India Meteorological Department ( IMD ) . <p> " ENSO-neutral conditions are likely to persist until July and there 's a probability it might move towards El Nino conditions , " he said on Saturday ( January 31 , 2026 ) , during his monthly @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ <p> An El Nino is defined as the Central Equatorial Pacific heating , on average , at least half a degree warmer than the average for five consecutive overlapping three-month periods . Historically , six out of 10 El Nino years have been linked to depressed rainfall over India . The El Nino is the converse of the La Nina - a half degree cooling - and the two are cyclical phenomena . The last global El Nino was during 2023-24 , a year that saw rainfall below normal levels in India . <h> Risk of sub-par monsoon <p> The latest climate models show a greater than 50% chance of an El Nino appearing over India after June and increasing to nearly 70% during July , August , and September . July and August are the most important months of the summer monsoon . <p> Jatin Singh , chairman of private weather forecasting company Skymet , said that " early " climate models signal a likely El Nino in 2026 , raising the risk of a " sub-par monsoon and drought conditions over India . " <p> El Nino forecasts @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , Mr. Mohapatra said , adding that those made in April are generally more accurate . <h> Dryer , hotter February expected <p> The IMD forecast said that below-normal rainfall is likely over most parts of the country in February , except in some areas of northwest and east-central India . Temperatures over most of India are likely to be on the higher side , except parts of southern India , the IMD added . <p> Rainfall in January was 31% lower than normal for the month . <p> " It 's been observed that snowfall during the winter months has been reducing ... probably due to the impact of climate change , " Mr. Mohapatara added . <h> Comments <p> Comments have to be in English , and in full sentences .
